- **minimal**: Cache-aside is the default because it degrades well: if the cache is down, every read still succeeds against the database, just slower. Write-through couples your write availability to the cache — the cache is now on the critical path for correctness, not just speed. Write-behind trades durability for latency and is only acceptable for data you can afford to lose. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/distributed-caching-design/minimal) · ★2)
- **minimal**: ROC-AUC flatters an imbalanced model because its x-axis is the false positive rate, `FP / (FP + TN)`. With 99% negatives, `TN` is enormous, so thousands of false positives barely move the FPR. Precision, `TP / (TP + FP)`, has no such cushion — those false positives land directly in the denominator. A fraud model at 0.5% prevalence can post ROC-AUC 0.97 while its precision at usable recall is 0.08. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/model-evaluation/minimal) · ★2)
- **minimal**: ROC-AUC is optimistic under heavy imbalance: its x-axis is FPR, and with 99,000 negatives, 990 false positives is an FPR of 0.01 — visually negligible, yet those 990 swamp the 1,000 true positives. Precision-recall keeps that damage visible. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/imbalanced-classification/minimal) · ★2)
